## A plush avatar in game, 2026-11-14: dropdown names, a renamed parameter's default, the squash's Build pressed twice, the readout slot, the world probes. FIXED 2026-11-14/15, SHIPPED in 5.0.0
*Moved from `Unfinished.md` item 40 on 2026-11-18. From the plush build session after Joe's in-game
test. Commits: eae20dc3 (dropdown title and None), 7b4ca92d (renamed default), d63195a5 (world
probes removed), 9a1be5f3 (readout off by default).*

Status of the points that were not plain fixes, as of 2026-11-18:
- **Height slider: CLOSED 2026-11-15**, Joe: "it works", his own mistake. Its proposals (opt-in
  scaler, a child through a ScaleConstraint) are not taken; item 33 holds the scaler's future.
- **The tissue trailing with speed:** the speed patch shipped with item 41; since then an untouched
  part holds on its bones (item 49, caf63e98) and past 7 m/s hangs rigid (8b2fb4bb), so an untouched
  part no longer trails at all. What is left of it is the belly's stop, in item 41.
- **Upside down: not reproduced** (1 mm off rest either way on a breast pair and a belly; item 41's
  guards read 0.9 mm). The lift-along-world-up proposal is not built.
- Still in the queue under item 40: Smaller Arms doing nothing in game, the ask to expose `_Damp`
  and a gravity share under Feel, and the three minor ideas.

Read from code by that session, not yet reproduced here. Each needs a failing test (or an in-game check
where only the game can tell) before a fix.
- **Dropdown option 0 is named "Option 0": FIXED on dev 2026-11-14 [M].** In a VRChat menu value 0 has no button (a toggle going
  off returns the int to 0), so `ScanIntUsage` seeds `{0, null}`, `UsefulStateName` drops the off state's
  name ("Off", "Default", "New State") and `BuildIntEntry` falls back to `$"Option {i}"`. Proposed: label 0
  "None" when the parameter has menu uses. All 15 dropdowns on that avatar. Option 0 with menu uses and
  no useful state name now reads None.
- **A dropdown is titled after its first option: FIXED on dev 2026-11-14 [M].** `FriendlyMenuName(paramUses[0].DisplayName, ...)`,
  so a "Tops" submenu became "Hoodie" and "Eye Expressions" became "Left Wink". Proposed: the parent
  submenu's name (`WalkMenu` already carries `prefix`). Done as `DropdownTitle`: an Int whose menu uses set
  two or more values takes the deepest submenu they share; a lone toggle, or uses with no submenu in
  common, keep the old name. `DropdownNamingTest`: WRONG before (Hoodie, Left Wink, Option 0), PASS after.
- **"The height slider is busted": CLOSED 2026-11-15, Joe: "it works", his own mistake.** The injected scaler's Size layer
  writes the root's `m_LocalScale`. The session suspects it fights ChilloutVR's own scaling and leaves the
  viewpoint behind; but per the decompiled universal scaling (memory, 2026-11-10) moving the slider is
  meant to flip the client to legacy animation scaling, where height follows the animated root. Needs
  what Joe actually saw (camera, colliders, the slider snapping back?) before a fix. Also: CVR sliders
  have no range, so the menu shows the raw 0..1 as "45". Proposals from the session: opt-in scaler, or
  scale a child through a ScaleConstraint. Joe's call.
- **Live tissue trails further the faster the avatar moves [R; G Joe: "if I move very quickly, it
  stretches super badly"].** `Ahead()` damps `vh`, the home's own velocity, by `exp(-_Damp*dt)`, so
  `_Damp` acts as world drag on the body's motion, not only on the jiggle: the session estimates the
  front trails by about `_Damp*V/w^2`, 22 to 33 cm on a belly at 10 to 15 m/s. Bounds today: the 8 m/s
  snap and an absolute 1 m Fresh reset (not scaled to the avatar). Proposed: damp `v - vh` only; clamp
  the lead to a scale-relative limit; scale the Fresh distance.
- **Upside down the tissue sags double [R; G Joe: "when I'm upside down, the jelly behaviour is busted"].**
  `_Lift` props the shape along the anchor's own up while gravity is world down: upright they cancel,
  inverted they add (about 11 cm on a belly), lying flat the pull goes diagonal. Proposed: lift along
  world up, as Cored already does, or fade to it as the anchor turns over. Also asked: expose `_Damp`
  and a gravity share under Feel, since a rebuild overwrites hand edits.
- **The debug overlay ships as a material slot: DONE on dev 2026-11-15 (Joe: "turn it off by default,
  debug only when its needed").** A plug's or socket's *In-game readout* tick is off for new ones; an
  existing plug keeps its tick until unticked and re-baked.
- The session's stopgaps on its own side: option 0 renamed and dropdowns retitled, scaler off,
  `_Damp`/`_Gravity`/`_Lift` zeroed after each build, the overlay slot trimmed.
- **A renamed parameter starts at 0 whatever its menu says: FIXED on dev 2026-11-14 [M].** `RenamePass`
  ("Hide Arm Fluff" to `HideArmFluff`) runs before `ApplyParameterDefaults`, which looked defaults up by
  the VRChat name, so the controller kept 0 and the avatar loaded on the wrong toggles until the game
  applied the saved menu. Now the CVR menu entries' own defaults fill in by machine name.
  `RenamedDefaultTest`: 2 WRONG before, PASS after.
- **Pressing the squash's Build again killed the jelly and made the upload take half an hour: FIXED on
  dev 2026-11-14 [M].** A rebuild was meant to write over its own copies, found them by the slot's plain
  name (`<avatar> Body 0.mat`), and after the first build they were numbered ("Body 99"), so it never
  did: it built new copies and left the old ones stripped, the jelly taken off (lattice and parts zeroed)
  and the squash compiled back in beside it. The prefab, and the upload, still wore the old ones: the
  belly never moved, and the body shader with squash and jelly both cost about 25 minutes of compile.
  Reproduced on a copy of the avatar's project with today's toolkit and the user's older one alike; with
  the fix each slot's previous copy is written over in place and the avatar stays jelly-only (Roles 24).
  "Bake every plug and verify" was checked too and touches neither.
- **What the squash costs an upload [M].** One body material alone in a bundle, empty shader cache:
  jelly only 31 s, squash only 520 s, squash and jelly 766 s. The squash's contact loop was written out
  four times; rolled, fxc on one vertex variant goes 14.8 s to 7.1 s (9655 to 5772 instructions). Every
  other unrolled loop in it was measured and left alone (no gain). A jellied avatar carries none of the
  squash code at all, which the Build fix above keeps true.
- **Live tissue's world probes removed (Joe 2026-11-14) [R from the client].** They were physics balls,
  non-trigger colliders under the hips. ChilloutVR turns those off in the wearer's own copy and back on
  in everyone else's on the remote player layer, and sets every avatar child's layer at load: they felt
  nothing for the wearer and shoved other players with an uncapped spring. A trigger cannot be pushed
  and an avatar cannot read an overlap, so there was no layer or trigger fix. Gone from avatars and props
  alike, with the step's solid-face code.
- **The speed patch ships with item 41 (Joe 2026-11-15: "keep it"); the upside-down one stays out.** Upside down did not reproduce on a
  breast pair or a belly (1 mm off rest either way). Damping only the motion apart from the home's
  halved the breasts' settled trail at 5 m/s (123 to 62 mm) but made the belly's worse (80 to 96 mm), and
  a constant 95 mm offset after a stop in every run is suspected to be the harness's. Open.

## The optimisation pass of 2026-11-17: first upload, first view, conversion. DONE and measured, SHIPPED in 5.0.0
*Moved from `Unfinished.md` item 48 on 2026-11-18. Joe, 2026-11-17: "full optimisation pass", then
"yes" to items 1-6 and the cache measurement, then "throw all optimisations into 5.0.0".*

Left in the queue under item 48: what was queued and not built (one state texture for all slots,
the lighting keywords from Unity's built-in sets, the empty asset refreshes, orphan clips, the
rehomer's texture writes) and the looks nobody has had in game (Shader Filtering on the rolled
lattice loop, point lights on an older Poiyomi avatar, upload time on the tester's machine).
"Kar's slow tissue step" below is closed: it was the 10-31 spike's shader in Kar's test scene, not
the shipped code.

**Folded into 5.0.0** (Joe 2026-11-17: "throw all optimisations into 5.0.0"; main 4077a2e4, tag moved, draft packages
and notes updated). Cold first upload A/B on the plush, Library/ShaderCache cleared before each bundle build, one run
each: 5.0.0 140.4 s, these changes 124.0 s; YAPS and JellyStep compiles 122.3 to 106.2 s, every other shader 9.2 and
9.3 s (the control); bundle 27.62 to 27.57 MB. Two read-only reviews of the folded commits found nothing.
**The idle pass folded too** (Joe 2026-11-17: "shove it all in 5.0.0"; main 03962bd8): vertex light per pixel,
the soft bake's presses side by side, the VRCFury rehome's material batch, the texture list's ties. Test build rc19
(same contents) went to a tester with the in-game checks; nothing of it has been run in game yet.
**The tester's rc19 conversions** (Xeon E5-2673 v3, 24 threads, RX 5500 XT; their own avatar with squash and
tissue, 2026-11-17): first convert 148 s, 51 s of it recompiling the 13 patched files the update made stale; the
third 54 s against 133 s for the same avatar on the 11-16 build (squash 104 to 42.7 s, FollowSwaps 35.9 to 5.7 s,
animator merge 17.7 to 5.4 s), 0 errors. Upload time not recorded in the files.
Four read-only reviews (first view, per-frame GPU, conversion, upload). Per-frame GPU had little left after
item 36. Done on dev, each measured:
- **Outline skip missed two lock forms** (e29f2013, test 84547ce8): older Poiyomi locks write a zero width bare,
  `clip(OutlineMask * 0.0 - 0.001)`, Pro as `float(0)`; both now left unbent like the commented form. The
  OutlineClipTest reproduced it first (bare and float(0) WRONG) and passes now. The reviewer's estimate: about a
  third of an affected patch's first-upload compile (Zombunny 2: 18 s of 85 s wall), plus a dead vertex pass a frame.
- **The tissue step's stereo variants** (95b5e3dc): its `skip_variants` never reached the ones Unity adds, so 9
  variants compiled where 3 are drawn. YapsStereoStrip drops every stereo variant of `Hidden/AvatarBridge/JellyStep`
  (only ever blitted). Proven on a Kar bundle: the step's funnel now ends 4 -> 16 -> 4 after scriptable
  stripping (Zombunny before: 3 -> 12 -> 9). Open: those 3 compiled fp variants still took 161 s wall / 224 s CPU
  on Kar, against 9 in 10 s / 57 s on Zombunny on 11-16. Not this change (it removes variants only); another
  Unity may have been compiling at the time, and Kar's step variants differ. Worth one quiet re-time.
- **GoGo poses in one batch** (b2b9a1c1) and **no second controller save after the slot filler** (3b5cdf34):
  measured by the 5-avatar subset run `opt-sub5` (below).
- **The tissue's 8 lattice corners in a rolled loop** (6691fdbf): fxc 51,844 to 43,388 bytes at 3 slots; the plush's
  body vertex programs 48.8-49.5 KB to 40.4-41.1 KB a pass. Cut short by Shader Filtering, the region is drawn at
  rest rather than torn. The first check counted the corners, and fxc proved the count 8 and dropped it, so a
  filter cutting the loop in bytecode would have torn the skin anyway; it reads the weights' sum now (696a6c94),
  which survives in the listing (`ge ..., 0.999` then `if_nz`), +196 bytes. Not run under the filter: Joe in game.
  Kar's tissue numbers identical.
- **No marker-light search in a plug's shadow caster** (e2b3231c), which is handed no lights: the caster program
  86,988 to 80,500 bytes. Bend tester identical, PlugGhostProbe PASS on every route (lights-only included, via the relay).
- Plush vertex bytecode a viewer builds: 535 KB to 456 KB (two body passes now byte-identical, 19 to 18 distinct).
- **The driver keeps built shaders across restarts** (NVIDIA, this machine): the same bytes in a fresh process
  built in 49-76 ms against 2,790 ms for new bytes. So the first-view freeze is paid once per machine per exact
  bytecode, not per session: byte stability across avatars and versions is worth more than kilobytes.

Queued, larger: one tissue state texture for all slots (fxc: -11 KB a body pass at 3 slots, ~100-145 ms first view;
risk, several steps writing one pair); lighting keywords the host's vertex never reads multiplying the YAPS vertex
variants. **VERTEXLIGHT_ON done on dev 2026-11-17** (7d6f71f3): a host declaring it for both stages gets it per
pixel when its own vertex programs compile to the same bytes with and without it (four light sets, the
material's keywords, 0.35 s a host); later Poiyomi already declares it so. VertexLightTest: a pixel reader is
rewritten, a vertex reader is not, and under a vertex light the pixel stage still sees the keyword. All six
Poiyomi 8.1 hosts tried are rewritten. Cold upload of an avatar with two such patches: 286 to 238 variants,
22.3 to 20.6 s of YAPS compile, bundle 43.9 to 41.4 s (one run each). LIGHTPROBE_SH and the ForwardAdd axes
come from Unity's built-in sets, which cannot be split by stage without redeclaring them: not done.
Not run in game: Joe, point lights on an older Poiyomi avatar look the same.
- **Kar's slow tissue step was not the shipped code** (re-timed quiet 2026-11-17: 4 variants, 214 s wall,
  403 s CPU). Kar's test scene still draws the 10-31 spike's `Assets/AvatarBridge_JellySpike/JellyStep.shader`
  (four copies of the step share the name in Attempt Conversion); the tool's own in YAPS/Generated/Jelly built
  in about 5 s on the plush. Nothing to fix. Smaller,
queued: the empty asset refreshes per avatar (attribute first), the orphan clips left in a merged controller,
batching the rehomer's texture writes.

## Live tissue stretching while moving: rigid in fast flight, no longer leading the body, held on its bones until touched. DONE 2026-11-17, SHIPPED in 5.0.0
*Moved from `Unfinished.md` item 49 on 2026-11-18. Joe, 2026-11-17: "disable squash and jelly in
fast flight mode", then a tester on rc19: "the physics still stretch like last time or more", then
Joe: "let magica handle all physics ... squash and jelly only handle mesh deforms". Commits
8b2fb4bb, db9574e8, caf63e98 to ce9e95b5; folded into 5.0.0 at main e32abbc8.*

Left in the queue under item 49: why two passes lag a frame, the tester's answer on which stretch
they meant (the cloth's own swing under the tissue), and that none of it has been seen in game.

Measured in the stability harness (`-jellyStab`, Kar's breasts, a glide eased in over 30 frames).
- **Fast flight, DONE on dev** (8b2fb4bb): past `_Fast` (7 m/s) the tissue hangs rigid, eased in from 5 m/s, off
  the smoothed speed so every copy agrees. Sprint (4 m/s) and walk are under it. At 10 m/s the old tissue sat
  65 mm off and stretched 2x the whole flight, then 209 mm on landing; now drawn at rest, 118 mm on landing.
  Film: before against after at 4 and 10 m/s (Dev/Release/tissue-speed-film.mp4).
- **The tissue led the body, DONE on dev for one pass** (db9574e8, then one-pass only): the solid squash
  fitted last frame's particles and mass against this frame's anchor, read a frame's move as a stretch and fed
  it back. Cloth off, steady at 90 fps: 18 mm ahead at a walk, 53 mm at a run, now 0.8 mm. Under 72 fps
  (two whole passes) the same fix left the front a frame behind (60 fps 22 mm, 25 fps 65 mm against 15 mm), so
  there it keeps this frame's anchor, which happens to cancel that lag. **Open:** why two passes lag a frame.
  Each pass moves the particles exactly the anchor's half step; the shape holds compressed (back layers
  ahead, front behind), and `_Solid` amplifies it (0.44 to 0.73 cells at 60 fps). Taking the first pass's
  homes half a step back made it worse (60 fps 1.01, 25 fps 2.48 cells; the 60 fps hitch tore 5.6x) and was
  reverted.
- **Open, probably the tester's "stretch": the cloth under it.** Cloth on, a steady 4 m/s run turns the breast
  bone about 60 degrees outward and 10 cm sideways (the masses 34 to 44 cm apart), and the skin goes with it;
  73 mm and 2.1x stretch at the back face, unchanged by `_Solid`, `_Motion`, `_MassHz`, `_Pair`, `_Smooth`
  and `_Landing`. **Physics A/B on Kar (`-abSpeed 4`), swing peak in degrees, original PhysBones against
  the MagicaCloth copy:** 1.4 m/s walk 47 against 23-26, 4 m/s run 71 against 61. The conversion swings less
  than VRChat, so the swing at a run is the avatar's own physics (pull 0.18, spring 0.8, limit 50 degrees),
  not a loose conversion. Whether the tissue riding it looks worse than VRChat's skin on the same bone is
  open; waits for the tester's answer on which stretch they meant.
- **Folded into 5.0.0** (Joe 2026-11-17: "number 1"): main e32abbc8, tag moved, draft packages and notes
  updated; test build rc20 has the same contents.
- Harness flags added: `-jellyStabClothOff`, `-jellyStabSet name=v`, `-jellyStabDump f,f` (particles on the
  stepped anchor, the plane's distances, the region on the body), `-jellyStabSide`, `-jellyStabFilm`.
- **The physics move, the tissue only touches, DONE on dev** (caf63e98 to ce9e95b5, Joe 2026-11-17: "let magica
  handle all physics ... squash and jelly only handle mesh deforms"). The tester's rc20 avatar had live tissue,
  squash and a MagicaCloth chain on the same breasts and butt: the tissue's own swing rode the cloth's. Now an
  untouched part eases onto its bones over `_Hold` (0.4 s) and holds there (Ease 1, particles at home, mass
  reset), stepped and scanned so a touch wakes it next step: a hand in reach, the wearer's own HAND into the
  box (arms and the avatar's own colliders lie in the box at rest, 41.y bits 0 and 8 on Kar, and dent through
  the draw alone), a probe, a grip, or the pair 2 mm past their built distance (exactly at it, float noise
  let the hold go every frame while standing). Harness, hold off against on, steady: walk 1.82x to 1.05x,
  run 2.10x to 1.08x, 25 fps 1.59x to 1.00x, worst over a whole run 3.81x to 1.09x; still and kick
  unchanged; a hand pressed in 64.7 mm both ways. A part no cloth swings no longer swings at all, as on the
  source. The squash's stretch in motion is removed (shader, homes, setting, setup fold). Not in game yet.

## Squash anywhere, points 5 and 6: the soft body over the whole skin, and a bone physics system on the blitter. 5 SUPERSEDED by item 29 (the live tissue), 6 ANSWERED, NOT PLANNED 2026-10-27
*Moved from `Unfinished.md` "Squash anywhere" (Major Ideas), points 5 and 6, on 2026-11-18. Points 1 to 4
stay in the queue as the 5.1.0 plan.*

Point 5 was the thinking before the jelly spike. Joe's order of 2026-10-31 (item 29, "all parts of
jiggle on the avatar, whole body") replaced it: the live tissue is built per painted part and shipped
in 5.0.0 as a BETA. Its open questions (cost, remote copies, DynamicBone pairs) were worked in items
25 and 29; read those records, not this one, for what was measured.

**5. The soft body over the whole skin, the brush choosing what is soft.** Joe, 2026-10-27: "expand
that system to include the entire skin of the body, and the paint brush can determine which parts
actually move and squish, similar to the MagicaCloth paint tool". Item 25 bakes the tissue per pair
and keeps its answers per vertex. Its answers-by-place variant (a box of cube corners per part,
not adopted for its memory) would not care what a part is called, but costs about 5 MB a pair, so
a whole skin needs it sparser. Thinking so far, nothing built:
- The painted weight decides the tissue: free where painted, held where not, the way the shares
  hold the tissue behind each part today.
- Pairs become islands: each painted island gets its own presses (straight in and a few slanted
  ones round its surface) in place of the eleven ways a pair has, and the shader reads a hand
  against the island nearest it.
- Bake time is about a second a press, so a belly, thighs and arms add up to a minute or so. Each
  island's box is a few hundred KB to a few MB.
- Open: islands pressing each other (a thigh into the belly when sitting), which is the pair's
  partner logic made general; and how the shader picks islands without a loop over all of them.
- Open, Joe, 2026-10-27: how far it can be optimised, which decides it; local and remote players
  (the squash already runs on every viewer's own client from bone positions and the hands the
  client hands every shader, so no sync); two avatars' soft parts close together (point 2); and a
  pair on DynamicBone instead of MagicaCloth2: the squash reads only where the part bones are,
  but the colliders press option and the follow points under DynamicBone need checking.

**6. A whole bone physics system on the blitter. Joe, 2026-10-27: "we can make a
physbones/magicacloth system? all of it???" Answered, NOT PLANNED.** Possible in principle: the
blitter's self-update keeps state frame to frame, a material data provider feeds it the bones, and
the hands come from the client's globals. But it is draw-only: nothing writes a bone back, so
whatever hangs on a bone (props, lights, particles, contacts, a renderer whose shader is not
patched) stays where the bone rests, and every material on the moved skin needs the patch. A
viewer with the Cameras content filter off loses the components, so the hair would freeze; Shader
Filtering cuts the solver's loops; the blitter on remote copies is still unproven. MagicaCloth2 is
built into the client and moves real bones, and physics match the source. The part worth having,
soft tissue that answers everyone's hands without colliders on their side, is point 4 and item 25.

---

## The `_Stretch` plan's review: 17 corrections. FOLDED INTO THE PLAN 2026-10-17
*Moved from `Unfinished.md` "PhysBone `_Stretch` on ChilloutVR" (Major Ideas), its "Judge's corrections"
section, on 2026-11-18. The plan stays in the queue, not started. Kept because each line is a claim the
first draft made and the reason it was wrong. `Unfinished.md:NNNN` cites are as of 2026-10-17.*

1. **Missed: `_Stretch` is also motion-driven.** VRChat's Stretch Motion makes bones stretch from movement (VRChat PhysBones docs). Marshmallow PB reads a gauge PB's `_Squish`/`_Stretch` from motion (`Unfinished.md:2004-2010`). The original step 1 ("act when the animator reads `<P>_Stretch`") would have put grab boxes and 32 bits on gauge chains. Added a grab-driven filter, a decision row and a survey caveat.
2. **Wrong order: the merger's reference set does not exist when it is needed.** PhysBones runs before the merge (`BridgeConverter.cs:172-174`) and destroys the PhysBones (`PhysBoneConverter.cs:431-447`). `AnimatorMerger.cs:4980-4994` is `PruneOrphanedParameters` working on the merged master. The helper is `CollectReferencedParameters` at `:5008`. Rewrote steps 1 and 3: check the source controllers, and capture the data before the PhysBones are destroyed.
3. **"Grab fields are not read today" was half wrong.** `allowGrabbing` is already read at `PhysBoneConverter.cs:457`. Only `PhysBoneData` skips it. Also corrected the `PhysBoneData.cs:72-76` citation, which points at the MaxStretch/Parameter fields, not a reader.
4. **`HoldUnlatchedContacts` is not a hold-after-release pattern.** It makes a pulse "on while touched, off when it leaves" (`ContactsConverter.cs:399-439`). Corrected.
5. **`SyncRoom` never skips.** It only appends a report warning (`YapsSocketReactions.cs:225-240`), and the true tally exists only after the merge. Reworded the budget step.
6. **Missed: the add-on package's Core list.** A new Core helper must be added to `YAPS_CORE` (`Dev/Build/yaps-files.sh:16-20`; `build-package.sh:119-123`), or the add-on fails to compile on its own, as in 4.1.0 and 4.1.1.
7. **Missed: the second builder.** `PhysBonesInPlace` (the Toolkit "PhysBones to cloth" card) runs `PhysBoneConverter.Run` with no merger, so it needs its own preserve handling.
8. **Missed: existing Core helpers for the shipped controller.** `AvatarSurvey.ShippedController` and `CvrSetup.SharedController` already exist in Core and in `YAPS_CORE`.
9. **The `grab` pointer is contested.** The CCK docs call it legacy and active "on the hand that is currently grabbing an object". The beta decompile says "until the player's first fist". Widened G2 and added a pickup case to the spike.
10. **Dropped the "~1 mm steps".** That figure describes the YAPS channel. The trigger record says 32-bit floats carry "no quantisation worry". The 10 Hz figure is kept and labelled a doc statement.
11. **Core parameter count is 24, not 22**, per both the docs page and `CvrParameterNames.cs:77-84`.
12. **Data.cs claim re-sourced.** The cited tree URL does not show `Data.cs`. The raw file confirms `// Pose,` in `GrabState`. Also noted that `_Angle` is described two different ways (`GrabbyBonesSupport.cs:9` against `ForkPlan.md:74`).
13. **Report surfaces were incomplete.** Added `ReportGrabbableChains` (`PhysBoneConverter.cs:474-478`), DynamicBone's **Skipped** line with GrabbyBones off and "Max Stretch is not converted", and the README line saying grabbing is mod-only.
14. **Survey detail.** Stay tasks already count as writers (`AvatarSurvey.cs:434-445`), so a written `_Stretch` clears the finding on its own. Removing `_Stretch` from `Scaffolding` would also surface gauge chains. Flagged.
15. **The PhysX-joint route was understated.** Joints really stretch bones (add-on, `Unfinished.md:1576-1610`). What stays open is a hand physics collider, which is [GAME], not "nothing grabs". It stays ruled out for pull.
16. **Converter default precedent.** `syncSocketDepthForOthers`, the same 32-bit contact-to-synced trade, defaults off. The "On" recommendation is now Joe's call, with that precedent shown.
17. **Smaller fixes.**
    - The trigger value spans the whole box, from face to face, Box shape only.
    - `EnsureTrigger` uses `ZNegative` and forces interaction on, so mirroring the filter is new code.
    - Corrected line ranges: `GrabbyBonesSupport` path, `EnsureTrigger :627-674`, `YAPS5 :19-21, :33-36`, `AvatarSurvey :902-905`, `BridgeElements` path.
    - The note.com article is flagged by its author as outdated, so "common build" is [R].
    - VRChat "springs back on release" is not stated in the docs, so it is [R].
    - Added `allowPosing` as the natural default for "Stays open when let go".

## The mesh tools plan's review: 19 corrections. FOLDED INTO THE PLAN 2026-10-17
*Moved from `Unfinished.md` "Mesh tools in the Toolkit" (Major Ideas), its "Judge's corrections" section,
on 2026-11-18. The plan stays in the queue, not started. Kept because each line is a claim the first
draft made and the reason it was wrong.*

1. **Re-run GUID conflict.** The plan saved through `OutputAssetPaths.Claim` and also promised that re-runs keep the GUID. Claim deletes the file at the path, and its own comment says "The GUID changes" (OutputAssetPaths.cs:53-66). Claim is now first-save only, re-runs go through the SaveInPlace pattern, and the required `Reset()` call is added (:31-36).
2. **The add-on's file list was missing.** The add-on carries Editor/Toolkit but only the `YAPS_CORE` Core files (yaps-files.sh:16-21). Added the rule that every callee must be on that list, and checked that the named ones are.
3. **Wrong reason for the add-on calling the brush core.** "The add-on depends on the public package and never the reverse" is wrong: the add-on contains Editor/Toolkit itself (yaps-files.sh:26), and there is no asmdef. The conclusion stands, with the right reason.
4. **Put back is not ready to use.** `Tool()`'s putBack label is hard-coded to "Put the textures back" (ToolkitWindow.cs:322), and `canPutBack` is called unguarded (:309, :323). A small change to `Tool()` is needed.
5. **SkinFrame was underspecified, in four places:**
   - The matrix must be evaluated at the play (rest) pose. "SkinFrame⁻¹(posed)" was ambiguous.
   - Weights must come from `GetAllBoneWeights` (AnimatorMerger.Physics.cs:749-753).
   - The repo's two BakeMesh callers disagree on `useScale` (YapsSquashPainter.cs:445-453 vs HeldShapeFrameProbe.cs:186-193), so the spike fixture now includes a scaled renderer and a vertex with more than four influences.
   - Normal deltas must be renormalised against unit rest normals, the 4.6.2-4.6.4 torn-plug lesson (`DirectionDelta`, YapsBaker.cs:928-932). "Rotation × normal" alone is wrong under scale.
6. **Split/combine verdict reversed to adopt by default.** BlendShapeEditor (free) does merge, concatenate and L/R split. An unstated licence blocks bundling, not users using it. Joe's rule points to adopt. "SmoothRange" is not on its page, so that claim was dropped.
7. **Blend Shape Modifier corrected.** "NDMF-only" holds (NDMF >=1.13.0), and package.json has no VRChat SDK dependency. Its README does not list a split feature.
8. **More existing tools added.** Lattice's Move and Smooth brushes and BlendShape output, with its package.json showing no SDK. Polybrush (free, official, deprecated from 6000.3, skinned support unclear). VRCMeshDeformer's "Save as BlendShape" and its no-bundling terms. Easy Mesh Editor's mirrored keys. MorphMixer. Chillaxins' short known-working list. Starmesh was checked: CVR-tested, but it has no pose capture or split.
9. **PoseMesh restated.** It is a corrective sculpt in a pose, not a pose-to-shape converter. The "no pose capture" claim is now marked as search-based.
10. **The CVR viseme facts were incomplete:**
    - The CCK has `SingleBlendshape` and `JawBone` lip sync modes (CVRAvatar.cs:20), which are an existing fallback.
    - WireFace never writes `visemeMode` and can change `bodyMesh` and blink (CvrSetup.cs:297-298, 312-326).
    - The Toolkit already has a Face card that calls it (ToolkitWindow.cs:488-490).
    - The CVR docs warn that visemes fail if an animation uses the same shapes, so the card always writes new shapes.
    - `DetectVisemes` needs only 7 of 15 (:200), so the test now checks all 15.
11. **Cats details.** Its inputs are exactly A, O and CH. Each viseme is a one- or two-input linear mix scaled by an intensity (tools/viseme.py:97-99, 103-180, 258-284), not "up to three". Cats is MIT.
12. **UnifiedBlendshapes cannot offer L/R names without a Core edit.** Its table is private (:34), only `BuildPlan` is public (:123), and it holds face-tracking names only. Plain suffixes are used instead.
13. **Split simplified.** It uses the painter's posed bake (:427, :445-447). SkinFrame is not needed there.
14. **Painter mechanics completed.** The dab is geodesic Dijkstra over the welded graph (:524-555), which is the painter feel. The welded adjacency already exists (`_near`, :210-222), so it is [M], not [R]. A position brush must rebake per dab. Citations corrected: weld folding :210-222, OnScene :489-522.
15. **ShapeWriter simplified.** Appending needs no clear-and-rewrite; only replacing a shape does. A clashing name must be refused, because AddBlendShapeFrame does not create a new shape for an existing name.
16. **Path and line fixes.**
    - Paths: YapsDebugOverlayBuilder, YapsComponentEditors and YapsSquashPainter are under Editor/Yaps/Setup. CCK_CVRAvatarEditor.cs is in the project's CCK, not the repo.
    - Lines: the frame loop is :546-556; the held-weight pattern is :186-197; the GrabbyBones text is README.md:798-803; the last-frame fold is :941-972; WriteFolder is :253-258.
17. **Avatar cost note added.** AvatarWeight counts only clip-bound shapes as animated (:149-184) and warns past 64 unanimated ones (:410-414). Corrective and viseme shapes count toward that warning.
18. **Tests changed.**
    - Fingerprint: `YapsSquash.Fingerprint` hashes positions only, so the non-destructive test needs its own hash.
    - Added a re-run identity test.
    - Packaging: compile-check runs five combinations (:10-11), and a passing compile there now covers the add-on's file list.
    - Test gate: `CVR_CCK_EXISTS` only.
19. **Decisions added.** Two new rows: split/combine adopt-or-build, and how visemes are wired.

## The Toolkit package plan: the add-on defect it found, and the review's 11 corrections. DEFECT FIXED 2026-10-17 (eef78599), moot since 2026-10-26 (ff6616fb removed the card); CORRECTIONS FOLDED INTO THE PLAN
*Moved from `Unfinished.md` "ChilloutVR Toolkit as its own package" (Major Ideas) on 2026-11-18: order
step 1 and the "Judge's corrections" section. The plan itself stays in the queue, not started.*

**The defect.** The plan's closure emulation flagged `SoftBodyContact` for the add-on's file list: the
add-on shipped `Editor/Toolkit` whole, the soft body card called `Editor/Core/Physics/SoftBodyContact.cs`,
and that file (and the `Editor/Core/Physics` folder entry) was not in the add-on. `git tag --contains
1598869` was empty, so it had not shipped broken. Fixed 2026-10-17: the file and the folder entry
added to the add-on list, and an add-on plus MagicaCloth2 compile gate added. The file and the folder
entry left again on 2026-10-26 with the soft body card itself. Checked 2026-11-18:
`Editor/Core/Physics/SoftBodyContact.cs` does not exist and `Dev/Build/yaps-files.sh` does not name it.

**The corrections** (each a claim the first draft made, and why it was wrong):

1. **The claim that no ChilloutVR optimizer exists was wrong.** NDMF Merge's README points to NDMF Avatar Optimisation (MilchZocker, MIT, ChilloutVR, build-time, non-destructive). It partly overlaps the weight and free-wins cards. I also added Chillaxins, which brings NDMF and Modular Avatar to ChilloutVR. I added d4rkAvatarOptimizer as VRChat-only. Sources: github.com/MilchZocker/NDMF-Avatar-Optimization, docs.hai-vr.dev/docs/products/chillaxins, github.com/d4rkc0d3r/d4rkAvatarOptimizer.
2. **"The CCK warns, this card fixes it" was only partly true.** The CCK itself auto-fixes missing scripts, oversized textures and streaming mipmaps, which are three of the things "Check before upload" refuses (`UploadPreflight.cs:45-50`). The line holds only for audio and stereo shaders.
3. **The plan's closure check would abort the Toolkit build on a false positive.** `UploadPreflight.cs:56` names `YapsNativeBuilder` inside `#if AVATARBRIDGE_YAPS`. I confirmed this by running the check's logic on a copy in scratch, without running the build. The plan treated the check's blindness to `#if` as harmless. I added the fix (leave `Editor/Yaps` out of the absent-type scan in toolkit mode) and noted that the check never looks at `Runtime` types (`build-package.sh:153`).
4. **The SoftBodyContact defect is now shown, not just reasoned.** The same scratch run flags `SoftBodyContact` for the add-on list, and `git tag --contains 1598869` is empty. The fix also needs the `Editor/Core/Physics` folder entry. Otherwise that folder gets a GUID that clashes with the public package's (inferred).
5. **FaceTracking (decision 3) does not need `CvrSetup` split.** The folder has no code. Its only route in is `CvrSetup.Run`, whose only caller is `AvatarBridgeWindow.cs:1047`, which the Toolkit package cuts. So the default flips to "drop it". The add-on carries it today with no way to load it either.
6. **The Avatar Tester needs a DynamicBone shape.** It has `AVATARBRIDGE_DYNBONE` blocks, so I added a `toolkit:` shape for it. Its textual closure is clean, so it now counts as checked, not just a reader finding.
7. **Option D's blocker now has outside evidence.** The existing ChilloutVR UPM package references only NDMF in its asmdef and reaches `CVRAvatar` by reflection. The Toolkit uses `using ABI.CCK.Components` directly.
8. **"Import order does not matter" was not true for README.md.** Each package swaps in its own text at the same GUID, so the last import decides what the project shows. I added this to C and to the contents of `README-toolkit.md`.
9. **The menu-rename cite was wrong.** `:157-166` are other menus' `ExecuteMenuItem` strings. The Toolkit's own path is used at `Dev/Tests/AvatarBridgeSmokeTest.cs:492`.
10. **Several line numbers were off.** compile-check's CCK-only shape is `:60` (not `:61`). The 4.1.x history is `build-package.sh:117-123` (not `:123-127`). The README-yaps "never removes" text is `:43-45`. The `toolkit:` shapes also need edits at compile-check `:67` and `:122`.
11. **Some tags were upgraded from [R] or reader finding to [M].** The `ConvertPhysBones` setter (`PhysBonesInPlace.cs:22`), the `PhysBonesInPlace.cs:1` guard and the `HasAvatarBridge` check (`BridgeLinks.cs:27-29`) were all read directly. The line counts (69,920 and 15,197) were measured. The README's "ships in the converter's own package" was already incomplete, since the add-on carries the Toolkit too.

## YAPS 5: the plug follows a path, not a point. BUILT: the socket list in 4.6.0, portal and Two At Once in 5.0.0 (2026-11-18)
*Moved from `Unfinished.md` "YAPS 5: the plug follows a path, not a point" (Major Ideas) on 2026-11-18.
Its own status line was stale a second time: it said portal and duplicate were not built. They are:
the plan of 2026-10-17 (`Closed-2026-10.md`, "Portal and duplicate: the plan") was built on portal-prod,
merged 2026-10-19 (e7fc97f9; 131537be Two At Once), kept protocol 9 by putting the exit in the atlas's
top rows, and shipped in 5.0.0 with README sections "Portals" and "Two at once". What is still open
stays in the queue under "The atlas: what has never been worn": deepest-plug-wins where a socket's
shapes ride the animator, a ring and a hole across two bodies in game (B5), and the portal's game gates.
The design below is the 2026-10-15 reading, with its withdrawn marks, kept for the reasoning. The
contact channel it costs out was deleted in 4.5.1.*

*Status: the ordered list is BUILT and shipped in 4.6.0, and so is deepest-plug-wins on the
shader route. Portal, duplicate and deepest-plug-wins on the animator route are not.*

*The other half of YAPS 5, how a plug FINDS a socket, lives in `YAPS5.md`, which gathers every
transport, every measured limit, and the order they get built in. This section is what the plug
does once found.*

**This entry said "planned, unstarted" until 2026-09-12, months after the thing it asks for
shipped.** The ask was an ordered list of sockets with arc-length ranges instead of one socket,
and that is `YapsChain` in `yaps_resolve.cginc`: `position`, `forward`, `kind` and a cumulative
`arc` *(WITHDRAWN 2026-10-17: there is no arc field, `yaps_resolve.cginc:45-63`)*, filled nearest-first, a hole ending the path, with `yaps_deform.cginc` selecting the link
per vertex by how far along the shaft it sits. Joe caught the stale status by knowing the feature
existed. A status line nobody rereads is worse than no status line, because it is the one thing
a planning read trusts.

| want | how it falls out | state |
|---|---|---|
| a ring mid-shaft *and* a hole at the tip | two entries in the list | BUILT, shipped 4.6.0, untested in game across two bodies |
| portal: in at one socket, out of another | two entries with a gap between their ranges | NOT built: the cascade is continuous, one Bézier per consecutive pair, and nothing can express a gap |
| duplicate: the shaft showing in two places | the same range mapped twice | NOT built |

A vertex shader cannot create geometry, so a "duplicate" is the existing shaft drawn at two
frames rather than a second shaft. It cannot be two different lengths. **"It costs nothing" was
wrong** (corrected 2026-10-15): one vertex has one position per draw, so the second frame needs a
second copy of every pass the plug's material has, shadow caster included *(only if the passes are
doubled inside the shader, 2026-10-17: a second draw of the shaft gets every pass from Unity)*. That is the plug
drawn twice, which is cheap for one plug and not nothing.

**Design, 2026-10-15, not built, needs Joe's decision.** Read off the shader as it stands:

- **The deform half is small.** A portal is a link whose segment has zero length: the cascade
  in `yaps_deform.cginc` (~798) already walks `fromWorld`/`fromForward` link to link, so a portal
  link starts the next segment at the exit with the exit's outward facing instead of the
  entrance. Triangles straddling the cut would stretch across the room, so the rings within a
  short span either side of the cut collapse to the socket point. A triangle with two corners on
  one point has no area and draws nothing. Inside the body anyway, so nothing visible is lost.
- **The resolve half is the cost.** The plug finds sockets by scanning the atlas cells around
  itself, about 1.2 plug lengths (`yaps-atlas-reach-is-the-grid`). An exit across the room is
  never in that scan, so the ENTRANCE has to carry where its exit is. Today an octant is four
  pixels (position, facing+kind, tags, owner) and every extra pixel per octant widens the rect by
  eight per column. An exit position and facing is two more: six pixels, a wider rect, and a
  protocol bump. **Protocol 9 shipped in 4.7.2**, so this is protocol 10, and a 4.7 wearer and a
  5.0 wearer would be blind to each other until both update. *(WITHDRAWN 2026-10-17, twice over:
  protocol 9 shipped in 4.6.4, read off the tags; and protocol 10 follows only if the exit rides
  in the octant. The plan below puts it in the atlas margin, which no reader draws or reads, and
  keeps protocol 9.)*
- **The light tier cannot carry it at all**: one socket per light path is the whole budget.
  Portals are atlas-only, and the README would have to say so.
- **Authoring:** a socket field naming its exit socket, both on the same avatar, written by the
  avatar's own atlas writer, which can see both bones *(at build time only: at draw time a
  writer knows its own matrix alone, `YapsAtlasSocket.shader:78`, so the exit needs a writer of its own)*. A portal between two people is out: neither
  writer knows the other's exit.

Questions for Joe: is a portal worth protocol 10 in 5.0.0 (the major is the cheapest moment for
the break)? Is duplicate worth a doubled plug draw? Neither can be judged without two clients in
ChilloutVR, so neither is built blind.

**Multiple plugs per socket is a separate, much smaller job.** The bending already works:
every plug resolves sockets in its own shader, so two plugs into one socket needs no change
at all. What breaks is the socket's *shapes*: a ChilloutVR trigger writes from whichever
sender touched it last, so two plugs fight over the depth instead of the deepest winning.
Socket-side, no extra sync, and it can ship on its own.

**What the platform gives and charges for.** Unity hands a mesh four vertex-light slots and a
socket takes two, but the third slot is spoken for by the tracker light of whatever enters the
socket, and that tracker lives on a prop or on the other person, so it can never be counted at
build time. **The light path therefore carries exactly ONE socket, not two.** Two lit sockets
plus a tracker is five lights for four slots, and Unity fills by range, so the casualty is
always the lowest range in the protocol: the hole root at 0.4130, behind a ring root at 0.4230
and two fronts at 0.4530, with the tracker on top at 0.4930. An avatar over the budget loses its
holes and keeps its rings, which is exactly how this reached us. A portal pair costs two sockets
and so cannot ride the light path at all; it needs contacts or a script.

The contact channel is where bits get expensive: eight values per socket, 96–288 bits per plug
by tier, doubled by a second socket. Give socket two its own lower tier (engagement and
position, orientation dropped first).

Socket depth stays one parameter per socket, local by default and synced at the wearer's
choice. That is deliberate: sharing a slot
between sockets assumes one is engaged at a time, which is exactly the assumption
multi-socket and portal exist to break.

**Order to build it in, with what has happened to it since:** deepest-plug-wins first (small,
self-contained) is done where the socket's shapes ride the shader, `yaps_socket.cginc:102`, and
still open where they ride the animator, because a ChilloutVR trigger takes the last sender rather
than the deepest. The socket list is built and shipped. Portal and duplicate as ranges on top
remain, and the cascade would have to learn to express a gap first. The converter repointing an
author's reactions onto YAPS's own depth, once last on this list, shipped in 4.3.0 with the
rebuild.

---

## Marker lights are a broadcast, and the room is listening. REFERENCE, nothing open (the offset lifted 2026-09-08)
*Moved from `Unfinished.md` "Marker lights are a broadcast" (Major Ideas) on 2026-11-18. It was never work:
its own status read "reference: measured facts, kept for design work; the distilled limits are in
`YAPS5.md`". The rule to carry into any design: a plug may declare its own length, a socket never gets a
"let toy mods read me" control.*

Added 2026-08-17, after a user reported their controllers vibrating whenever they came within
two metres of a converted avatar. Everything here was learned from source, and it changes what a
socket is allowed to be.

**A marker light is the only thing an avatar does that reaches into someone else's.** Its range
is a message: every decoder reads `range % 0.1` and matches 0.01 hole, 0.02 ring, 0.05 front,
0.09 plug tip, and anything on the platform may listen. Raliv's shader matches within 0.005; a
toy mod reading the same protocol in C# matches within 0.001. VRCFury authors +0.0006, inside
both, which is why a stock converted avatar drove a stranger's toy across a room. YAPS authored
+0.003 from 2026-08-23, which DPS read and that mod did not; that lifted on 2026-09-08, see the
work queue entry.

**Toy integration cannot be made safe from the socket side, and that is not a bug here.** The mod
computes reach as `1 - distance / giver.Length`, and estimates `Length` from whichever renderer
sits first under the avatar root rather than reading the length DPS states in the tip light's
intensity. A socket is only ever the target of somebody else's number. So:

- **plug side, one day: yes.** A plug can declare its own length honestly, by giving the tracker
  light a sibling whose mesh states the measured length. The mod climbs to the first renderer
  under the light, so an inactive one on the marker object is enough, and then it engages within
  a plug length rather than a room. That is a switch that risks only the wearer.
- **socket side: never.** Any "let toy mods read my sockets" control hands strangers the right
  to decide how far away they can reach you.

[ddakebono/CVRGoesBrrr#2](https://github.com/ddakebono/CVRGoesBrrr/pull/2) bounds the estimate
with the stated length, and it is in the mod's latest build, which is what allowed the offset to
lift on 2026-09-08. Both notes above still hold for the plug side: declaring length honestly is
the wearer's own risk, and a "let toy mods read my sockets" control still hands strangers the
decision, so there is none. Builds of the mod from before the merge stay broken either way.

**A tempting idea that does not work: shrinking the ranges.** Since only `range % 0.1` is read,
0.0106 decodes exactly like 0.4106 and reaches a fortieth as far. It would ease vertex-slot
pressure, but Unity ranks the four per-object light slots BY RANGE, so a tiny light is the
first evicted by every stock DPS avatar in the room. It would work alone and fail in company,
which is the opposite of what a compatibility feature needs. Worth keeping only as a "YAPS talks
to YAPS and nothing else" mode, where this decoder sets the rules.

---

## Two walls the transports hit, and whether a shader goes round them. ANSWERED: the authority gate DISPROVEN in game 2026-08-22, the atlas BUILT (4.5.0), light colour SHELVED; the 512-pair cap stands
*Moved from `Unfinished.md` "Two walls the transports hit" (Major Ideas) on 2026-11-18. Every question it
left open has an answer since: the GrabPass survives an upload and works across avatars in game; the
atlas it calls "designed and unbuilt" shipped in 4.5.0 and is at protocol 9; cell collisions are handled
by the hashed cells, two homes and the tag (`YAPS5.md`); light colour as data was demoted on 2026-08-22
and shelved (`YAPS5.md`, "Light colour as data": lights are legacy-only and legacy plugs cannot read
colour; revisit only if fallback pressure appears). The grab's editor cost is kept below; the open
in-game figure went to the queue under "The atlas: what has never been worn". The 512-pair cap and the
"tags are the lever" rule are in `YAPS5.md`.*

**WITHDRAWN, in game 2026-08-22:** the first wall below, "a prop can only be driven by your own avatar".
Cross-avatar prop writes work; the toucher's client syncs them (`YAPS5.md`). The reading is kept so
nobody rediscovers it from the same decompile.

Read out of the client 2026-08-22 while costing a contact-based replacement for the marker-light
channel. Both of these are platform behaviour, not this tool's code, and both bound what any redesign can
achieve.

**A prop can only be driven by your own avatar.** Every contact write into a `CVRSpawnable` value
goes through `TriggerToContact.HasProbableAuthorityToApplySync`. It allows a sender that is
another prop (if either is synced by you), a sender that is *your own* avatar, or the world. There
is **no branch for another player's avatar**, so a remote sender falls through and returns false
and the value is silently never written. This is very likely a client bug rather than policy: the
prop branch falls back to `IsSyncedByMe()`, and that fallback is simply missing for avatar
senders, so even the prop's own syncer is refused. It predicts the split chased here:
avatar-to-avatar works (the gate only runs for spawnable triggers), old DPS props work (lights,
never this path), YAPS props work on your own sockets and never on someone else's. **Test before
building anything around it, and report it upstream if it holds.**

**512 overlapping contact pairs, instance-wide.** `CollectPairsJob` caps `pairs` at 512 and re-adds
*previous* pairs first, so an established interaction is sticky and cannot be evicted, but in a
saturated instance a new one may never register: "works once I am in it, will not start in a
crowd". The broadphase itself is brute force over every sender for every receiver, but Burst and
parallel with a squared-distance test, so it is sub-millisecond and not the constraint. Rejections
on tags, `contentType` and owner happen *before* a pair is written, so **tags are the lever on the
512, not volume count.**

#### So: is there shader magic?

Two routes, and the cheap one is much more interesting than the famous one.

**The light colour is free, and nobody is using it.** A vertex light hands the shader
`unity_LightColor` alongside its position and range. These markers are black with non-zero intensity
(zero intensity drops a light from the per-object list entirely), so three channels per light are
sitting unused. If a root light's colour carried the socket's axis, a YAPS-native socket would
need **one** light instead of two, and with the tracker holding a slot, that is three sockets in
the budget instead of one. Legacy plugs still need the root+front pair, so this is a YAPS-native
mode alongside the compatibility pair, not a replacement. Cheap to spike, and the open questions
are small: whether CVR's asset filter clamps light colour or intensity on avatars, and how much
precision survives the intensity multiply.

**SPIKED 2026-08-27 IN PLAY MODE AND IT WORKS.** A quad writing a known value into a fixed corner
of clip space, a named `GrabPass`, and a second quad sampling it back: the value returns exactly.
The grab comes back **ARGBHalf**, 16 bits of float per channel, worst error measured at 0.00005,
which across a two metre range is about **0.1 mm**. That is twelve times finer than the contact
channel, arrives every frame rather than ten times a second, and needs no smoothing, so none of
the resolution-versus-lag trade above applies to it at all. `Assets/YapsSpike/` and
`Assets/Editor/SpikeAtlas.cs` in the Dracaionan project.

Writing the patch in clip space, ignoring the object's transform and the eye, makes it
stereo-proof by construction: both slices of the eye texture array get identical content, so
there is no double-wide layout maths to get wrong. This is the part that does not survive
conversion from VRChat, and it is simply not available here.

**Still unanswered, in order:** whether ChilloutVR's asset filter keeps a `GrabPass` through an
upload (only an upload can say); the per-frame cost of a named grab in VR; whether the patch
escapes frustum culling when the socket is off screen (scaling the quad enormously works, since
the vertex shader ignores its position and only the bounds change); and cell collisions.

**The grab's cost, measured in the editor 2026-10-29** (`Dev/Probes/Perf/GrabPassPerf.cs`, RTX
4080, D3D11, ARGBHalf, no MSAA, GPU held busy so the copy cannot hide): a named grab adds 0.067 ms a
camera at both VR eyes' size (4032x2240), 0.030 at 1440p, 0.016 or less at 1080p, and 8 readers of
one named grab cost what 1 does (+0.079 at VR size). An unnamed grab copies once per reader (8
readers +0.224 ms at 1440p). The 4080's 64 MB cache holds most of the screen it just drew; the
screen copy is about 144 MB of traffic at VR size, so a card with a small cache pays its full
bandwidth, roughly 0.2 ms on a 3080 and 0.6 on a 1070 (estimates, not measured). Still open: an
MSAA camera resolves before it grabs, and only CVR in game gives the real figure per camera (eyes,
portrait, CVR camera, mirrors).

**A possible answer to collisions, untested:** give each socket a build-time random id, hash it to
a cell, and write the id into the cell beside the position. A colliding cell then decodes to some
other socket's position, which is almost always metres away, and the engagement range gate already
rejects that. Collisions would degrade to "no socket found", falling back to lights or contacts,
rather than "wrong socket found".

**Original note, written before the spike:**

**The screen-space atlas is real, and it could be written better than SPS, but do not build it
yet.** Sockets render a small quad encoding their world position into a reserved screen region;
the plug samples it back through a named GrabPass. It is the only channel that dodges *both* walls
above: no light slots, no contact pairs, no parameters, so no authority gate and no sync bits, and
no cap on socket count. And the reason SPS's does not survive conversion is one that need not apply here: theirs is written for VRChat's double-wide, this would be instanced-native from the
first line (`UNITY_DECLARE_SCREENSPACE_TEXTURE`, the same family the shader patcher already
applies). Against it: a named GrabPass is a real per-frame cost; the atlas quads must escape
frustum culling; and the unsolved one is **cell collisions between avatars**, because two avatars
cannot negotiate which screen cell they own without scripting, and a collision is a wrong socket
position rather than a missing one. It also reintroduces the screen dependency that made the
contact route attractive in the first place.

**Order of work:** the light-slot fix first (done), the gate test (done, disproven), then spike light
colour. The atlas stays designed and unbuilt until something forces it.

## The physics night of 2026-10-07: Joe's orders, the fixes, and the grab gap research. DONE 2026-10-07 (1598869b, shipped)
*Moved from `Unfinished.md` "A mod-side GPU soft-body system" (Major Ideas) on 2026-11-18, where it had been
left under the idea Joe raised the same night. The idea itself stays in the queue. The soft-body
collision option and its Toolkit card named below were removed on 2026-10-26 (ff6616fb). The GrabbyBones
upstream PR is Joe's own to post (plan in `Regression/GrabbyBonesForkPlan.md`). The `_Stretch` plan in
the queue cites the gap research below.*

Joe's decisions before leaving it running: commit to dev and deploy to all 8 projects, **no release**
(main waits for him); **one** full throttled corpus run after the fixes; the soft-body collision
option ships **off**; GrabbyBones fork is **research and a written plan only**. He also wants the A/B
videos for Discord if the results hold up (captions name no avatar; the footage shows paid test
avatars, so posting is his call).

Found while he watched the live view: Avatar D's breasts clipped. Two causes, both fixed: the
soft-body spring floor had been bypassed for every `MC2_Preset_Bridge_*` preset (it was meant for
user-saved ones, but those share the shipped file's name, so the floor is back for all), and the
soft-body collision radius came from the chain measurement's narrowest cross-section, 2 mm on a
breast whose bones carry the front skin; it is now the median distance from the collision bone to
the mesh it carries.

Fixed tonight, each measured on its own (runs n0 to n5 in the scratchpad `physab/`): PhysBone 1.0
stiffness (`AsVersion11`, exact); Multi Child Type Ignore rooted at the children (no hinge at the
waistband); squish as bone-length stiffness (rigid without squish; presets had shipped it soft);
hand and finger colliders written as spheres (a capsule under a hand stops ChilloutVR adding the
hand collider that pushes other people's cloth, and on a finger gets it filtered out); the
soft-body collision option and its Toolkit card.

Gap research (decompile of Joe's beta client, so unverified unless noted): **pushing works natively**,
ChilloutVR adds players' hand colliders to every MagicaCloth2 cloth (Joe confirms he pushes cloth in
game all the time); a native grab is possible as an aim driven by the "grab" contact tag but costs
about 97 sync bits per chain, which Joe rejected (four chains would eat the budget), so grab, pose,
stretch and squish go to a **GrabbyBones fork** (plan in progress); `_Angle` is possible natively
with a constraint rig and a parameter stream (32 bits per chain); `_IsGrabbed` approximately with a
"grab"-tag trigger (1 bit; the tag starts on at load until the player's first fist); `_IsPosed` not.

## Conversion speed: the two passes and what 2026-11-17 added. DONE, SHIPPED in 5.0.0 (2026-11-18)
*Moved from `Unfinished.md` "Conversion speed: what is left (2026-11-16)" (Minor Ideas) on 2026-11-18: the
finished and the tried-and-dropped parts. What is still open stays in the queue under the same title.
Commits checked in main: 16f1a73d, 936bfcc8, e5bea683, 307fbe9d (merged as aa35180d).*

The first pass compiled each squash shader once, skipped the soft bake when
the jelly takes every dented part, and batched clip and emote writes. The second pass (on dev,
2026-11-16) remembers each shader copy across conversions and restarts (Library/AvatarBridge),
skips squash spread on skin far from every part, re-patches a jelly's materials in one batch,
sets animator transitions and behaviours only when they changed, caches the material audit per
renderer and property, and finds GoGo clips with one search. Measured from each step's
times in `Diagnostics.md`.

- **The soft bake's presses side by side** (16f1a73d, 2026-11-17): each press on one thread and a tissue
  of its own, in place of ~100 small `Parallel.For` calls per substep (~13 s a pair before). SoftBakeTest,
  a synthetic pair of 6782 particles: 20.5 s to 7.0 s, all 30 answers bit for bit. Not timed on an
  avatar: none in the corpus bakes now, the jelly takes every pair. The cache by input hash was not
  built and stays in the queue.
- **Volatile assets rehomed one forced import at a time** (`RehomeReferencedAsset`). **Measured and part
  fixed on dev 2026-11-17.** The step took 8 to 22 s on the large VRCFury avatars; a log line (`Rehome steps`)
  put all of it in rescuing the materials swap clips name (43 on one avatar, 14 of them copied), about 175 ms to
  write each and 310-350 ms in its textures. The writes now go in the scene pass's batch (936bfcc8): 7.8 to
  5.7 s and 13.4 to 8.6 s on that avatar, the same 243 output files. Setting motions only on a change measured
  nothing and was reverted. (What is left of it stays in the queue.)
- **The texture warning tie** (e5bea683, 2026-11-17). After the full corpus of 2026-11-16 (7a4d2163, 7 of
  88 changed, the release run 0), six scenes of one avatar listed two equal 5.3 MB texture warnings in
  the other order: `AvatarWeight` sorted by bytes alone, so ties followed material order. Now by name,
  then path.
- **Upload time, measured 2026-11-16** (Joe, 2026-11-16: "add the upload timing to the queue"). The
  CCK's bundle build compiles every patched shader's variants; fewer copies should help, nothing
  measured. A/B with `BundleBuildProbe` on one avatar, cold (salted include so Unity's shader
  cache cannot answer): the conversion from before the speed work against the current one.
  Measured 2026-11-16 instead, once, cold (Library/ShaderCache cleared, 535eae0e, 8 cores at
  BelowNormal): the bundle build took 100 s, 85 s of it shader compiles. The two squash body copies
  took ~28 s each (base, Outline and ForwardAdd passes ~9 s apiece), JellyStep 10 s (57 s CPU,
  already shader_feature_local). The A/B was dropped: the speed work leaves the final shaders
  identical, so it cannot change what the upload compiles. Levers left: fewer body copies (each
  role set is its own copy), or a cheaper JellyStep fragment.
  Optimize Mesh Data ruled out 2026-11-16 (a plush avatar, cold, same machine): on 174 s, off
  187 s, same 18.8 MiB. With it on, a mesh-data pre-pass compiled 1070 variants in 139 s; with it
  off the main pass compiled the same variants itself. Off the plush's 173 s of shaders, the plug
  shader alone is 72 s (1010 s CPU) and each squash copy ~20 s (156 variants each). Levers, in
  order: the plug shader's variant count, then the number and cost of squash copies. After 5.0.0.
  **Plug shader plan (read-only, 2026-11-16).** The cost is the vertex program, not the
  fragment: per pass 32 vertex variants at ~10 s CPU each (325 s and 359 s for two passes) against
  16 fragment variants at ~0.4 s. Four passes compile. The YAPS deform is the same in every
  variant; only Poiyomi's lighting keywords differ (from the plush's compiler logs: DIRECTIONAL,
  STEREO_INSTANCING_ON, VERTEXLIGHT_ON, SHADOWS_SCREEN, LIGHTPROBE_SH in the base pass; POINT, SPOT,
  the cookies and SHADOWS_* in ForwardAdd; SHADOWS_DEPTH/CUBE in the caster). No double-wide
  stereo variants compile, so the YapsJelly skip_variants trick buys nothing here; instancing and
  the vertex-light keyword are both needed in ChilloutVR. Steps, cheapest first:
  1. Measure where one vertex variant's time goes: fxc `/Zi /Fc` plus the per-function attribution
     script used on the jelly step, on one base-pass variant. The walk (8 steps unrolled, kept so
     because Shader Filtering cuts a late `[loop]`), the resolve's 3x3x3 atlas scan, and the chain
     loops are the suspects. Pass bar: name the function that holds over half the instructions.
  2. If it is a block written out several times (an unrolled step carrying its own copy, a
     function inlined at several callers), write it out once, as the walk's landing already is.
     The jelly step went 43.8k to 8.5k instructions this way.
  3. Fewer passes run the deform: ForwardAdd and the outline pass could take the base pass's
     deformed position only if the plug skipped them, which changes how it lights. Ask Joe first.
  Step 1 measured the same night (the plush session, fxc on the exact preprocessed base-pass vertex
  variant, Unity's own compile 2.6 to 3.8 s a plug variant; not re-run here yet): full 2589 ms /
  2808 slots, YapsDeform stubbed 65 ms, YapsResolveChain stubbed with its result kept live 899 ms /
  2019 slots. So the chain scan (3 scans x 3x3x3 cells x 2 homes x 8 octants, plus the 4-entry
  sorted insert into a dynamically indexed local array) is ~65% of the compile at ~28% of the
  instructions. By instructions nothing passes half (YapsResolveChain 20.5%, YapsDeform 15.1%,
  YapsWalk 7.3%, YapsSafeNormalize 6.3% inlined many times). Not a duplicated block, so step 2
  becomes: flatten the cell, home and octant loops into one `[loop]`, and keep the four nearest as
  named scalars with compare-swaps instead of the array. YAPS_CHAIN_MAX below 4 does not compile
  (indices written out for 4). Shader Filtering applies: the scan's loops are `[loop]`, so check
  the flattened one still resolves with the filter on.
  Step 2 done 2026-11-17: cells, homes and octants are one `[loop]` of 16 steps a cell. fxc on
  the stub targets: the resolve 2.33 to 1.40 s, the whole deform 2.50 to 1.96 s, temps 59 to 43,
  slots unchanged (3053 to 3050). Tried and dropped: the four nearest as named scalars (slower,
  deform 3.04 s) and the three passes folded into the same loop (5% more, 84 more slots).
  In Unity, `FreshCompileTimeTest` (one body variant per host with every role, one run each):
  Poiyomi Toon 65.3 to 59.9 s, Poiyomi Pro 54.6 to 47.9 s, YAPS Standard 67.9 to 63.9 s. The bend
  tester's 88 lines are identical before and after. Not yet: a cold bundle build of a plug avatar,
  and an in-game look with Shader Filtering on.
- **Parallel shader compiles: tried and pulled.** Handing every new copy's passes to the compiler
  at once measured nothing: in batch, Unity compiles a copy during its import, and the per-pass
  check after it returns in ~0 ms either way. The import is the cost (~10 s per 70 files here).

Nothing to fix. The PlayAudio watch moved to item 56.

0.6018086 against 0.5987598 for the same FBX and scene, ratio 1.005092; the same ratio showed in the hand rebuilt
squash's bindposes. Something in the GUI session left the avatar scaled by about 0.5% when the bake read it (the
Avatar Tester or the Height slider are the suspects). The bake should measure the avatar at its own size whatever the
scene is doing. 3 mm on 0.6 m, no visible effect in game.

**Closed 2026-11-18:** Not a Unity version difference: the plush and plug FBX imported in fresh 2022.3.22f1 and 2022.3.58f1 projects give the same bone scales, positions and bindposes to 7 digits. So the 0.5% came from something live in that one editor session (the scene and prefab on disk had no scale overrides), gone by the time it was looked at, and 3 mm on 0.6 m changes nothing in game. Reopen if a bake comes out a different length again, with the root's and the armature's scale read from the editor at that moment.

## 52. Code rot review. Joe, 2026-11-18: "a code rot review, or tech-debt review, we been writing a lot". DONE 2026-11-18

A whole-repo pass for what the 5.0.0 run left behind: dead code and settings nothing reads (the tissue's Give, Swing and
Spring back are out of the UI but still in the shader and presets), migration paths for marks only test builds wrote,
duplicated helpers between the converter and the toolkit, comments that describe removed behaviour, probes whose
flags no longer exist, and files past a size anyone can hold in their head (`YapsJelly.cs`). Report first, ranked,
then fix in small commits with the tests and corpus around them.

**Closed 2026-11-18:** Four reviewers (YAPS, converter and window, Dev tree, docs against code) and five commits: 668a18cf (docs and strings), c3e98586 (converter: dead methods, one copy of each helper, the RehomedAssets folder bug, the Toolkit's blink), 1d5eacd6 (YAPS: the jelly's HLSL in YapsJelly.Shaders.cs, one EnsureFolder, MeshOf and parameter naming), cf396546 (Dev: probes never slim source textures, script drift, 28 finished probes deleted), plus ca8b0b28 for the package menu. Decided and left: the shader-text cleanups wait for the next shader change (Minor Ideas, "Fold into the next intentional YAPS shader change"); the free-mass and sleep paths stay as probe paths; Give, Swing and Spring back stay hidden at the presets' values (Joe's rule of 2026-11-17: the cloth moves the part, the tissue only dents), comment corrected; the rc-only name fallbacks in YapsJelly.Unworn stay until 5.1.0, since Joe's and the testers' rc-built avatars still carry those names; the file-name sanitisers are not merged (a changed name moves output files and breaks their GUIDs); the controller-save duplicate between CvrSetup and AnimatorMerger, the Atlas and Baker save helpers, and the five editors in YapsComponentEditors.cs stay as they are (they differ on purpose, or the move buys nothing until one changes); probe-only members in shipped classes stay, like the converter's ForTest hooks.

## 32. The 5.0.0 review of 2026-11-06: what it left. DONE 2026-11-18 (point 12 built; the rest closed or moved to item 56)

Seven reviewers over every shipped file of main..dev; 29 findings held. Points 1 to 11 are fixed
or answered and shipped in 5.0.0 (record, and the three withdrawn findings, in
`archive/Closed-2026-11.md`). The numbers below are the review's own.

- Remainders of the fixed points. **6:** a user's own shader below target 4.5
   still reads the squash from the matrix slots; the patcher leaves its target alone. Whether the
   skinned slots now move the squash with the cloth on YAPS Standard: in game. **1:** the tester's
   copy as others see it now starts from rest (`TwinRestTest`); untested by hand in editor Play mode.
12. **Nested PhysBone chains on MagicaCloth 2** still move both chains, and the report names them.
    `MagicaClothKeepOut` now keeps ignored bones still; it could do the same for the nested chain.
13. **The live tissue dents a sparse part only broadly.** A deep poke moves the facing skin 2-4 cm on
    every part, but over the whole area under the hand. Where the mesh has no vertex within a
    fingertip's reach (measured: none within 3 cm of the poke and 2-12 within 10 cm, against 25-80 on
    a denser part), there is nothing to draw a finger-sized dent with, so it reads as no dent. Not the
    preset: the denser part's values pressed only 10-20% further. A finer dent needs either more
    vertices there or a dent drawn in the fragment stage.
14. **The live tissue's first compile: what the 2026-11-07 fix left.** The record, and two claims
    this item made withdrawn, is in `archive/Closed-2026-11.md`: a tissue shader's first compile
    is now two and a half to four times faster, and the check compiles a copy before reading its
    errors, so a shader that fails keeps that material on the squash alone, with a warning. Left:
    - A real timeout was never forced (a bloated include compiled 292 s without one). That the new
      check catches one rests on the corpus run, where a check after the wear's compile saw it.
    - In the editor window, Unity may compile a shader in the background where batch mode waits;
      the check now compiles it in place either way. Timed in batch only (first time 27-49 s a
      Poiyomi Pro material, again 0.7-1.1 s); in the window, untimed.
    - Each state read now takes four texture reads where one ran. Unmeasured in game.
    - The first-conversion times start to finish (notes, add-on card and film) are the corpus
      run's on the old code; a fresh one needs a corpus run.

**Closed 2026-11-18:** Point 12 built: a PhysBone nested inside another's chain is kept out of the outer MagicaCloth by attribute (MagicaClothKeepOut, as an ignored bone already was), so its own cloth alone moves it, as VRChat's Ignore Other PhysBones does; the report says so and README's limitation is gone. Compiled; the corpus run exercises it; the swing itself is item 56. Point 13 stays a known limit, not work: a part with no vertex within a fingertip of the poke has nothing to draw a finger-sized dent with; only more vertices there or a dent drawn per pixel would change that, and nobody has asked. Point 14: the window's untimed compile, a forced timeout and the four texture reads have no report behind them and are closed as unmeasured; the first-conversion times are refreshed by the next corpus run. Points 6 and 1 are in-game and editor checks, moved to item 56.

## 30. Pointers for the live tissue from a VRChat skin-touch module: what is left. DONE 2026-11-18 (point 2 fixed; ideas to Minor Ideas, looks to item 56)

DocMe Skin Touch 1.1.0 (free, Booth), read for ideas only: its licence bars its code, or a rewrite

joined, the press lit, heads pressing, the Firm preset (record in `archive/Closed-2026-11.md`, with
its withdrawals). The numbers are the item's own.

   of the prop's draw is the old rule.
3. **A press pushing the far side of a thin part: measured 2026-11-06, NOT BUILT.** The far side
   never moves; skin facing away is pushed only just past the flank by a palm deep in (8 cm limb:
   0.8 mm at 2 cm in, 2.3 at 3, 4.4 at 4; 12 cm: under 1 mm to 6 cm in), and `Held` stops a hand at
   about 2.7 cm on 8 cm, so about 2 mm at most. Build the facing ease only if a limb's flank is
   seen to bulge in game.

   is all but nothing. **Open:** a smooth-shaded avatar to see it on. The plug's press still lights
   as flat skin.
5. **A soft shadow as a hand nears: not built, large.** A darkening per vertex from the hand slots
   and own capsules the draw already reads, multiplied into the host's colour; needs a colour hook
   per shader family in `YapsShaderPatcher`, which patches the vertex stage only today.
6. Heads built. **Feet not built:** the client hands every player's feet, hips and chest
   (`_CVR_PlayerLeftFootPositions`, `_CVR_PlayerRightFootPositions`, `_CVR_PlayerHipPositions`), but
   not the foot's direction, so only an ankle ball would press. (~~"Feet do not port: the client
   hands a root, not feet"~~ withdrawn 2026-11-06.)
7. **The Avatar Tester's hand put on the skin by a click** in the Scene view (bake the body, ray,
   back off along the normal by the palm), and its controls in a Scene view panel. Not built.
8. Firm built, a first guess, untuned. **Left:** presets named by feel, not by part (maybe Thin;
   the enum serialises by index, so add after Firm), and saving a part's feel to a small file to
   share.
9. Smaller, none built: every problem at the top of the component with its fix; a line saying what
   the build adds; a cost measured on the user's own card; a pile-up ring round a deep press,
   draw-only; a dent whose rim grows with the press.
